Remark concerning F7:

	For F7, some definitions of the ModuliField will give Q(sqrt(2)) instead.
	This is because swapping F7 <--> 1-F7 interchanges dessins #1 <--> #2
	and dessins #3 <--> #4.

	The dessin of F7 has a Galois orbit of size 4, and thus the ModuliField
	has degree 4 in the classical definition of the ModuliField.

	However, if we count up to equivalence (where the dessins of F and 1-F
	are considered equivalent) then we have only two equivalence classes
	in the Galois orbit, and a definition of the ModuliField based
	on equivalence-classes of dessins (rather than the dessins themselves)
	will produce the field Q(sqrt(2)).

	Note that one can even give a model with F in Q(sqrt(2))(x),  provided that
	one allows the branching to be above three points p1,p2,p3 other than the
	usual 0,1,infinity.

	(take p1, p2, p3 := sqrt(3+6*sqrt(2)), -sqrt(3+6*sqrt(2)), infinity)

A1 has 1 dessin. The ModuliField is Q.
  g0 := [[2, 3, 4, 5, 6]];
  g1 := [[1, 2], [4, 5]];
